pocket_clearance:.2f}\",\n            param=\"shank_diameter\",\n        )\n    # Two chamfered mouths need more than 2 * chamfer_size of face between them, and a\n    # wall thinner than that also stops being a printable wall.\n    if wall <= 2 * chamfer_size:\n        reject(\n            f\"a {wall}mm wall cannot carry two {chamfer_size}mm chamfers: \"\n            f\"raise wall above {2 * chamfer_size}\",\n            param=\"wall\",\n        )\n\n    pitch = pocket_diameter + wall\n    width = (columns - 1) * pitch + pocket_diameter + 2 * wall\n    depth = (rows - 1) * pitch + pocket_diameter + 2 * wall\n    height = pocket_depth + floor_thickness\n\n    body = Box(width, depth, height)\n    top = height / 2\n\n    for i in range(columns):\n        x = (i - (columns - 1) / 2) * pitch\n        for j in range(rows):\n            y = (j - (rows - 1) / 2) * pitch\n            body -= Pos(x, y, top - pocket_depth / 2) * Cylinder(\n                pocket_diameter / 2, pocket_depth\n            )\n\n    if draft:\n        return body\n\n    # Only the edges lying in the top plane: the outer perimeter and the ten pocket\n    # mouths. Everything else is derived from it, so a set of 1/4\" hex bits or\n  a set of round 6.35mm shanks is one slider away.\n- **Pocket 6.3mm, shank plus 0.3.** That is the fits table's *moving* row, and it is the\n  right one here rather than *snug*: a bit is picked out and dropped back a dozen times a\n  day, one-handed, and 0.2 that slides on the printer that made it binds on the next.\n  0.5 would rattle and let the bit lean.\n- **Pocket 12mm deep in a 15mm block.** A 25mm bit stands with 13mm proud, enough to pinch\n  and lift, and deep enough that the bit cannot be knocked over. The 3mm floor under it is\n  the doctrine's wall minimum plus a margin, and it prints as solid layers rather than\n  infill.\n- **2.0mm of material everywhere between pockets and out to the sides**, which sets the\n  8.3mm pitch and both outside dimensions. It is the wall minimum, and it is also what the\n  kernel needs: two 0.8mm chamfered mouths want more than 1.6mm of face between them, so\n  2.0 clears the chamfer rule with 0.4 to spare. `wall` is guarded by `reject` for exactly\n  this reason, so shrinking it refuses instead of failing in OCCT.\n- **0.8mm x 45 degree lead-in at every pocket mouth**, and the same 0.8 around the top\n  outer perimeter so the block reads as one system. This is the one place the polish rules\n  bend on purpose: a chamfer at a mating mouth is normally forbidden, but here the mouth is\n  the whole user interface, a bit is aimed at it by feel, and the cone is what steers the\n  shank in. 0.8 is the doctrine's floor rather than the usual 1.0 because 1.0 chamfers on\n  a 2.0mm wall would collide.\n- **The bottom perimeter stays sharp.** A sloped chamfer arriving at the plate lays a knife\n  edge into the first layer, and the flat bottom is what makes the 15.0mm height exact.\n- Prints as it sits: flat bottom on the bed, every bore vertical and self-supporting,\n  nothing bridged. First layer is 809mm2, far under the 20,000mm2 where corners peel.\n- 8288mm3 solid, 40 faces, no slivers.\n\n## Don't\n\n- **Don't chamfer the bottom perimeter or the vertical corners.** Both were considered and\n  left off: the stated bounding box is exact only while the bottom is sharp, and the block\n  is small enough that a corner chamfer buys nothing a hand notices.\n- **Don't round the pocket mouths.** The lead-in is a 45 degree cone, the same facet as\n  every other edge on the part. A fillet there would print as a smeared rim on the top\n  layer and break the one-facet-angle rule.\n- **Don't chamfer the pocket floors.** They are concave edges: a chamfer there is a feather\n  edge, and it would also stop a bit from bottoming out flat.\n- **Don't thin `wall` below 1.6mm** to squeeze more pockets in. The part refuses, and the\n  reason is the chamfer landing rule, not tidiness.\n- **Don't add a lid, a label or a magnet.** Text is banned outright, and a magnet is a\n  pocket for a part nobody measured.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-24: first version. Ten pockets, 5 x 2, from the 6.0mm measured shank.\n\n```toml\n[part]\nmin_wall = 2.0\n\n[accepted]\nsliver = 0\n\n[variants.bit_block_3x2]\nnote = \"A shorter block: three pockets across instead of five, for a bench with less room.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_3x2.params]\ncolumns = 3\n\n[variants.bit_block_8x2]\nnote = \"A long strip of sixteen pockets, for a full driver set.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_8x2.params]\ncolumns = 8\n\n[variants.bit_block_hex_quarter_inch]\nnote = \"Sized for 1/4 inch hex bits, whose shanks measure 6.35mm across the flats.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_hex_quarter_inch.params]\nshank_diameter = 6.35\n```\nMD\nnurb build bit_block 2>&1; echo \"--- check ---\"; nurb check bit_block 2>&1","description":"Write card with variants, build and check all"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3136,"cache_read_input_tokens":53914,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3136},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"bad5915c-003d-4232-92ce-1398dbf0015e","uuid":"40757748-938e-4b3d-8559-da6739bf38cc","timestamp":"2026-08-24T21:16:42.139Z","request_id":"req_011CeNGU1MZcNkuWGxhzLWXi"}

Variants build at 3, 5 and 8 columns and at a 6.35mm hex shank.\n\nTwo judgement calls worth flagging, both recorded in `## Don't`:\n\n- **The 0.3 pocket clearance is the fits table's *moving* row, deliberately, not *snug*.** A bit gets dropped in one-handed a dozen times a day; 0.2 that slides on the printer that made it seizes on the next one. If you'd rather it hold a bit against a shake, `pocket_clearance` is a slider.\n- **The lead-in chamfer bends the \"never chamfer a mating mouth\" rule on purpose**, because here the mouth *is* the interface — you aim a bit at it by feel and the cone steers the shank in. It's 0.8 rather than the usual 1.0 because two 1.0 chamfers won't land on a 2.0mm wall; `wall` carries a `reject` that says so rather than dying inside OCCT.\n\nThe chamfers are there by design — pocket mouths and top perimeter only. The bottom perimeter and vertical corners stay sharp, which is what makes the bounding box exact.\n\nAbout 31 minutes and 9.2g of PLA on your P1S; `build/bit_block.3mf` is written with gyroid 10% and 3 walls embedded, so your slicer opens it tuned.
